# GT1-02-01 canonical account-root registration

Pre-task source HEAD: `63b1d466156804fffe70a308da96132c224bc693`.
Execution root and branch match [[CONTRACT]] and `run-state.json`.
Observed base `feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc`:
`882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`; merge base
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`; HEAD ahead/behind **8/10**.
No fetch, branch change or base update was performed.

Implementation: **implemented** for the three-file registry/resolver packet.

- `src/shared/providerProfiles.ts` registers `grok` with `GROK_HOME`, default
  `.grok`, and transcript subdirectory `sessions`. Existing account labels,
  profile keys and SSH host suffixes now apply. Whitespace-only roots use the
  existing `isBlankEnvValue` rule instead of inventing an account key.
- `src/shared/agentAccountDir.ts` resolves the winning environment layer before
  handling blanks. An explicitly blank agent/provider/global value cancels lower
  layers and selects the provider default, matching local launch's merge-then-unset
  behavior. Session env records still replace provider records; managed account
  bindings still win; remote paths remain remote and never expand against local HOME.
- `src/shared/envVarCatalog.ts` suggests only `GROK_HOME`, `GROK_AUTH_PATH`,
  `GROK_AUTH` and `XAI_API_KEY`. Descriptions distinguish state from independent
  credentials and describe the API key as a fallback, not a token override.

Source evidence is the phase 01 pinned official snapshot
`2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8`: `xai-dirs/src/lib.rs:33-70`,
`xai-grok-login/src/storage.rs:47-55`, `xai-grok-login/src/manager.rs:341-359`
and `xai-grok-pager/docs/user-guide/02-authentication.md:285-299` under
`crates/codegen/`. The already downloaded copies and receipts from
[[Plans/grok-tier1/evidence/01]] were reused. No provider binary, login,
credential file or network discovery was accessed. No unsupported gateway/model
environment name, capability or qualified version range was added.

## Scope and pending acceptance

Static diff review: **pass** for this packet, including independent review.
Deterministic tests, TypeScript checks and native qualification: **not-run**.
The phase contract separates implementation, test authoring and execution:
GT1-02-02 owns focused tests; GT1-02-13 owns their execution and type checking.
Parity outcomes remain unchanged pending GT1-02-14 reconciliation.

- Registering Grok activates generic account enumeration. Until GT1-04-01 wires
  the selected `accountDir` into Grok storage, per-account usage collection can
  reread the default root and duplicate totals. This intermediate branch is not
  ready for multi-account usage certification. GT1-04-02/04-13 must cover and
  verify distinct-root reads; GT1-04-11/04-12 own usage attribution checks.
- GT1-02-03/04 still own credential classification and login guidance. A state
  root is not proof of billing identity when inline/file/model credentials differ.
- Managed launch's `PROVIDER_ROUTE_ENV_NAMES` mapping still lacks Grok. The
  resolver honors a supplied managed binding, but registration does not establish
  launch pinning or removal of competing credential variables. Keep this blocker
  visible in GT1-02-14 and the phase 09 managed-route review before qualification.
- The existing remote resolver ignores local/global layers; desktop SSH launch
  separately forwards global settings. Their prior discrepancy remains unqualified
  and needs reconciliation during remote consumer/launch work (phases 04 and 10).
  No remote default was guessed from the local machine.

Only scoped formatting and diff hygiene checks are performed here. No test file
was authored, no suite ran, and no application store was initialized. Images
analyzed: **0**. Publication is a local task commit only; CONTRACT prohibits push.

## GT1-02-03 authentication guidance

Pre-task source HEAD: `dc9072e24deb8229b8876de9c16a476b5963f158`.
Execution checkout and branch match [[CONTRACT]] and `run-state.json`.
Observed base `feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc`: `882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`;
merge base `0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`; HEAD ahead/behind **10/10**.
No fetch, branch change, install, login or provider process was performed.

Implementation: **implemented** for the three named production files only.

- `src/shared/providerAuthIdentity.ts` distinguishes the verified Grok gateway
  overrides, deployment keys, opaque inline credentials, fallback API keys and
  login-capable file/OIDC/external-helper configurations. Deployment keys precede
  inline user tokens; current API-key names precede the legacy name. Blank values
  remain unsets. Explicit API-key lockdown or a team pin suppresses the fallback
  key gate. Classification returns names and safe labels, never inline JSON or
  malformed gateway contents; existing non-Grok behavior is preserved.
- `src/shared/agentMetadata.ts` selects `grok login --device-auth` on SSH and
  retains `grok login` locally. Existing custom-path substitution, shell quoting
  and host-scoped login-terminal ownership are reused without another command
  table or spawner.
- `src/renderer/utils/loginUrl.ts` recognizes `auth.x.ai` and `accounts.x.ai`,
  including the legacy sign-in route. Parsed host/path matching excludes host
  names embedded in queries or userinfo, keeps custom IdP path segments, and
  rejoins LF/CRLF wrapped URLs without joining adjacent URLs or multiword prose.

### Source evidence and limits

Reused the phase 01 cached public source at
`tmp/grok-tier1-environment/source/crates/codegen/`, pinned to
`2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8`:

- `xai-grok-pager/docs/user-guide/02-authentication.md:25-38,44-93,172-178,245-255,285-299`
  establishes local/device login, fallback API keys, proxy/OIDC/helper modes and
  model/token/key precedence.
- `xai-grok-login/src/auth_method.rs:13-52` supplies the legacy key name;
  `config.rs:124-174,304-310,411-435,498-517` supplies auth hosts and key lockdown/team
  pin semantics; `manager.rs:341-359` supplies inline-before-file behavior.
- `xai-grok-login/src/grok_auth_credentials.rs:3-5,54-60,96-105` establishes deployment
  key priority and its separate remedy. Config reference
  `xai-grok-pager/docs/user-guide/26-config-reference.md:181-192` supplies model/public
  API endpoint overrides.

These are source observations, not observed native login success or a certified
version range. No private credential/config file was read. The environment-only
classifier cannot determine selected per-model credentials, stored auth methods,
managed requirements, or ambient variables on an SSH host. A configured fallback
key is explicitly described as possibly inactive. Malformed inline JSON may fall
back to disk natively; the gate conservatively asks to correct/unset it without
parsing or exposing the bundle. Gateway selection is a deterministic guidance hint,
not a claim about the selected model's actual request route.

## GT1-02-03A conservative billing and account attribution

Pre-task source HEAD: `bc5db12f8f83594be3945be39d8fd675caebf7a7`.
Execution checkout and branch match [[CONTRACT]] and `run-state.json`.
Observed base `feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc`: `882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`;
merge base `0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`; HEAD ahead/behind **11/10**.
No fetch or branch update was performed.

Implementation: **implemented** in `src/shared/providerProfiles.ts` only.

- `resolveAgentBillingCredential()` returns null for Grok. An environment-only
  login gate cannot establish the selected model's billed account or justify
  excluding it from a plan quota. This covers fallback API keys, inline bundles,
  gateway hints and deployment keys; null means no proven override, not proven
  OAuth or quota participation. Existing providers retain their behavior.
- `resolveAgentProfile()` leaves Grok's non-OAuth classifications and independent
  `GROK_AUTH_PATH` overrides unattributed. It cannot merge unrelated inline
  accounts under `Inline credentials`, merge local/SSH hosts under a credential
  label, or turn a withheld billing credential into an assumed root login.
  No inline content is parsed, hashed, displayed or used to build an identity.
- `resolveAgentAccountKey()` and the canonical registry are unchanged. Profiles
  without independent credential hints still group the selected state root on
  its owning host, retaining blank/unset handling and distinct local/SSH keys.
  These are state-root groups only: selected model, file and managed authentication
  remain unqualified without an observation from the executing host.

Reused [[Plans/grok-tier1/DESIGN]]'s account/environment contract and phase 01/02
source evidence, including the pinned authentication guide's per-model credential,
active-token and fallback-key precedence and `manager.rs:341-359`'s inline/file
selection. No provider command, private credential file or operator store was read.
This packet introduces no observation API or capability promotion.

Implementation: **implemented** for the three named production files only.

- `src/main/process-manager/handlers/StdoutHandler.ts` replaces Grok auth messages
  for local and remote stdout failures with neutral credential guidance. It keeps
  the SSH UUID and names the remote host when supplied, including an unnamed
  remote fallback. No error text, credential value or local SSH executable is
  used to infer the active credential or a login command.
- `src/renderer/hooks/agent/useAgentErrorRecovery.tsx` gives Grok a **Review
  Authentication** action with conditional sign-in guidance. Its existing action
  ID and callback remain unchanged, preserving the `ReauthModal` path that owns
  credential classification, custom executable selection, host identity and the
  login terminal. Other providers retain their current actions and descriptions.
- `src/renderer/components/Wizard/services/wizardErrorDetection.ts` replaces both
  Grok auth messages and hints, so an unconditional login remedy from the pattern
  bank cannot appear above the neutral hint. Credential repair is scoped to the
  host running the agent; restarting the wizard remains the next step after
  correction. Detection, retryability and other providers are unchanged.

No fetch, branch change, provider command or credential read was performed.

Implementation: **implemented** in `src/renderer/hooks/stats/useProviderProfiles.ts`
only. The hook now tracks settled account inputs instead of requiring every agent
to have an attributed profile. When `resolveAgentProfile()` withholds a profile,
the existing `resolveAgentAccountKey()` determines whether a root is available.
A configured root can settle without home; an absent or blank root still waits
for home. The provider-environment fetch must still settle before `ready` is true.

The canonical resolver retains sole ownership of credential classification.
Fallback keys, inline bundles, gateways, deployment keys and independent auth
files stay unattributed; no credential content is inspected, logged, hashed or
turned into an unknown-account key. Known profile mappings/counts and local/SSH
identity construction are unchanged, including in a mixed fleet.

Static tracing confirms that the existing `AgentOverviewCards` effect can now
clear a removed root filter once inputs settle, including when all Grok profiles
are withheld. Its All providers view already includes agents absent from the
profile index. No component or shared resolver change is needed. Session env
replacement, the existing global-env boundary and other providers' behavior are
preserved. This readiness fix does not certify billing or managed/remote launch
attribution; those remain with their existing downstream owners.

### Coverage handoff and limits

GT1-02-03G owns tests in the existing `AgentOverviewCards.test.tsx` suite:

- Transition an existing root selection to every withheld Grok credential mode;
  after inputs settle, assert the removed filter clears and All providers shows
  the agent. Cover an entirely withheld fleet and a mixed known/withheld fleet.
- Defer provider env and home independently. Preserve a valid selection while
  inputs load; an explicit root needs no home, while blank/absent roots still do.
- Preserve ordinary providers, distinct local/SSH roots, env replacement and
  known-profile counts, with no placeholder profile for withheld sessions.

## GT1-02-05 scoped discovery foundation

Pre-task source HEAD: `db2797d91776c768601e93853fd0209bd88b805d`.
Execution root/branch match [[CONTRACT]] and `run-state.json`.
Observed base `feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc`:
`882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`; merge base
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`; HEAD ahead/behind **21/10**.
No fetch, branch change, native Grok invocation, login or provider network discovery
was performed. Images analyzed: **0**.

Implemented only in the three named production files:

- `src/main/agents/detector.ts`: accept explicit host-selected launch context;
  reject missing, remote/unresolved, relative-cwd and unqualified managed contexts
  before provider probing. Both a supplied managed environment and actual managed
  host standing refuse. Validate an explicit custom binary before general detection,
  build the effective environment and account root with canonical launch/account
  helpers, and carry selected cwd to the existing admitted probe. Read version
  through that runner; do not read native models_cache.json or credential files.
  Parse only the pinned `Available models:` section, preserve native IDs/aliases,
  deduplicate, and reject a malformed catalog as a whole. Failure returns only an
  explicit configured model when supplied, never guessed catalog/default IDs.
- Preserve the existing in-memory TTL mechanism only with a host-owned auth/config
  generation, keyed by executable/version/effective environment/cwd/generation.
  Keys are ephemeral SHA-256 of canonical JSON, never stored, logged or used as
  account identity. Only the latest Grok scope is retained; selected model is
  appended after cache lookup so it cannot contaminate another selection. Without
  a generation there is no Maestro catalog reuse; the native CLI owns its cache.
  Version is checked before cache reuse. No raw stdout/stderr, endpoints or
  credentials are logged; unexpected exceptions use a fixed sanitized report.
- `src/main/agents/capability-snapshot.ts`: strip Grok models/contextWindow on
  hydration and every write. The current provider/host key cannot represent the
  selected account/model/version. Binary readiness/path/version and other providers
  remain unchanged.
- `src/shared/maestro-lib/providers/definitions.ts`: reuse existing text/autocomplete
  controls for model and effort so stored/manual values remain visible without a
  qualified menu; remove the timeless two-model catalog, universal effort menu and
  claimed default-model context. Empty model/effort inherit the CLI's selection;
  context override defaults to 0 (unknown). Builders still pass explicit values.

### Evidence and limits

The phase01 pinned public source is
`2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8`, independent of candidate package 1.0.70.
The source/binary relationship and supported version range remain unknown. Source
inspection establishes an adapter design, not native certification:

- [Native cache](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/xai-org/grok-build/2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8/crates/codegen/xai-grok-cloud-config/src/remote_settings/cache.rs),
  SHA-256 `b0c6a18d83483f3b9b821dc6f32e5a309c38cdebd06c76839b42303e7d048742`:
  300-second TTL, 4 MiB limit, exact version/auth-method/origin/identity validation.
  Selecting GROK_HOME alone does not establish native cache eligibility.
- [Cache validation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/xai-org/grok-build/2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8/crates/codegen/xai-grok-cloud-config/src/remote_settings/validation.rs),
  SHA-256 `0830fe78fab6c46ee266ef514884d0d99b69f5f159efe4f89d6ffa95f8492e42`:
  scope includes session/API-key/deployment authentication, not just a state root.
- [CLI renderer](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/xai-org/grok-build/2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8/crates/codegen/xai-grok-pager/src/models.rs),
  SHA-256 `21ce0100ec9f527ca10b0f71bc3d5071942a9abfd5c8f3911c00056afacf6c66`:
  auth banner and default precede the available-model bullets. No effort/context
  metadata is supplied by this textual output. These three public artifacts were
  inspected read-only by the delegated reviewer; no account data was accessed.
- Phase01's cached `cli_models.rs` and [[Plans/grok-tier1/DESIGN]] document the ACP
  model-state source and acknowledged configuration requirement. Native projection
  owns model eligibility/aliases; Maestro does not reconstruct those from `info.id`.

The requested end-to-end discovery exceeds this packet's three-file boundary:
current IPC has no selected-agent/draft context, seven renderer callers omit it,
provider snapshots have no account/model scope, managed Grok environment vocabulary
is absent, and the generic probe runner has **no timeout** on either route. The
original assumption that the runner was already bounded was false. Legacy Grok
callers now intentionally receive no discovered catalog; model/effort text remains
editable. Actual remote catalogs, exact-binary identity qualification, per-model
metadata and a fully functioning scoped picker are **not implemented here**.

Under CONTRACT's continuation procedure, GT1-02-05A-F name the bounded remaining
implementation files, and GT1-02-05G-J name their regression suites before dependent

not claimed fixed. Existing GT1-03-10A/10B own acknowledged ACP model/effort/context
metadata and scoped remote publication; phase09 owns managed qualification. The
native candidate/live requirements remain with phase11. No test or production work
from these continuation checkboxes was executed in this task.

P03/P06/REG-MODEL/REG-DISCOVERY record these owners and blockers; implementation and

## GT1-02-05A - Grok discovery process limits

Date: 2026-10-30. Source: pre-task HEAD
`cf6c68ffcef3947b5d2ef1263868fdf551fe72b8` plus this task's local diff.
Execution root and branch were confirmed with `git rev-parse --show-toplevel HEAD`
and `git branch --show-current`. Merge base with
`feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc` is
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`; `git rev-list --left-right --count
feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc...HEAD` reported **10 base-only / 22 task-only**
commits. No branch switch, fetch, merge, or primary-checkout change.

### Implementation

- `src/main/agents/provider-probe.ts`: extend the existing request with optional
  `limits` and `signal`. Validate positive integral budgets and Node's timer range
  before admission. Opted-in standalone probes and admitted probes use the existing
  `startTurn` path, with `createIdleWatchdog`'s non-resettable `maxMs` ceiling.
  Both output streams share a UTF-8 byte budget; overflow discards collected and
  subsequent output. Abort triggers the shared termination ladder with the existing
  250 ms stop grace; completion awaits `turn.done`, with no Promise timeout race.
- Cancellation before admission starts nothing; cancellation during admission
  releases a granted lease without spawning. Synchronous start refusal/failure
  releases the acquired lease, while normal exits and asynchronous spawn failures
  remain owned by `startTurn`. Managed refusal stays `DispatchRefusedError` with
  no unmanaged retry. Non-opted-in standalone exec calls keep their exact arguments.
- Controlled timeout, cancellation, overflow, interruption (including authority
  revocation followed by exit 0), and process failures return no partial output.
  Diagnostic messages are fixed; synchronous startup errors are replaced with a
  fresh fixed error except for structured managed refusals. Cancellation reasons,
  environment values and native error text are never copied to these diagnostics.
- `src/main/agents/detector.ts`: Grok version probes request **5,000 ms / 16 KiB**;
  model probes request **15,000 ms / 1 MiB** combined output. These are host budgets,
  not native capability claims. Host-local `ModelDiscoveryContext.signal` reaches
  both calls; canceled/failed discovery preserves the configured model and does
  not populate the cache. Managed and remote Grok discovery remain withheld.

### Remaining lifecycle scope and verification handoff

These process budgets begin after admission; they do not bound host policy calls
or binary/path discovery. The existing shared lifecycle also prevents a strict
end-to-end completion guarantee: `startTurn.done` waits for stream close, whereas
`stopProcess` snapshots descendants only when stopping a still-running parent.
A wrapper that exits before its orphan closes inherited pipes can leave `done`
pending after the deadline. Separately, asynchronous Windows `taskkill` currently
has no helper timeout although its synchronous counterpart uses
`TASKKILL_TIMEOUT_MS`. Both are source findings, not exercised failures here.

CONTRACT's scope split adds **GT1-02-05A1** for the three canonical shared lifecycle
files and **GT1-02-05A2** for three existing lifecycle suites, immediately before
dependent GT1-02-05G. Ownership/PID safety, ordinary stream draining and exactly-once
lease release must survive that repair. Do not claim strict total probe completion
until those checks pass; no abandoned-process Promise race is permitted.

GT1-02-05G should cover finite/invalid limits, combined stdout/stderr overflow,
multibyte and unterminated output, fixed deadlines under continuous output,
pre-aborted and during-admission cancellation, zero-exit shutdown, revocation,
lease release on all paths, sanitized errors and unchanged legacy exec calls.
GT1-02-06 owns detector fallback/cache/cancellation checks; GT1-02-13 executes
those suites and the three suites named by GT1-02-05A2.

Independent static review: **pass** after fixing the controlled synchronous-error
sanitization gap. This is source review only. Test authoring, collection/execution,
TypeScript checks and native Grok calls are **not-run**, per this IMPLEMENT packet.
P03/P06/REG-MODEL/REG-DISCOVERY retain all acceptance/applicability statuses and now
name the remaining shared lifecycle owners. No native support or beta status changes.

established command-local hooks override after scoped checks, without changing Git
configuration or installing packages. Preserve and exclude `.sloptimize/`.
See [[GROK-TIER1-02]], [[GROK-TIER1-03]] and [[Plans/grok-tier1/DESIGN]].

## GT1-02-05B - Selected discovery context across IPC

Date: 2026-10-30. Pre-task HEAD:
`6d5dd8d6920563fd0d8d0b3fd0f7e39ed7b16922`.
Execution root and branch match [[CONTRACT]] and `run-state.json`:
`/Users/dr3/dev-shit/worktrees/auto-run-feature/grok-cli-integration`,
`auto-run-feature/grok-cli-integration`. Observed base
`feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc` is
`882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`; merge base is
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`.
`git rev-list --left-right --count feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc...HEAD`
reported **10 base-only / 23 task-only** commits. No branch switch, fetch,
merge, native provider invocation or production-data access was performed.

### Implementation and consumer contract

Only the three named production files changed:

- `src/shared/types.ts`: shared serializable `AgentDiscoveryRequest`,
  `AgentDiscoverySelection` and `AgentDiscoveryResult`. Requests name a provider
  and either a stored agent (`kind: agent`, `sessionId`), a draft, or provider
  settings. Draft/settings selections carry cwd, custom executable/environment,
  model/effort and SSH selection. No process environment, managed authority,
  cache key/generation or cancellation object is accepted as launch authority.
- `src/main/preload/agents.ts`: overload the existing `getModels(request)` and
  `getConfigOptions(request, optionKey)` APIs. Object calls return structured
  results; legacy string calls retain their original IPC argument positions and
  string-array types. Existing non-Grok handler branches are unchanged.
- `src/main/ipc/handlers/agents.ts`: one shared discovery handler resolves saved
  agents from the injected sessions store and provider/global settings from the
  existing stores. Provider mismatches, missing selections/stores, malformed env
  or SSH inputs, and missing/nonabsolute local cwd cannot select a host default.
  Current tab/run model/effort override saved agent values, which override provider
  defaults; empty tab/run choices inherit the saved agent. Unsaved provider settings
  may explicitly clear model/effort. Only plain string-valued environment records
  are accepted. The canonical desktop environment builder preserves session record
  replacement (including `{}`), global blanks/unsets and provider defaults; the
  existing detector owns canonical account-root resolution.

`resolveSshLaunchTarget` and its store adapter resolve host identity before any
local probe. Missing/disabled/unselected remotes and conflicting observed/configured
hosts return fixed unavailable reasons. Grok SSH discovery is withheld before
calling the old unscoped remote command or the local detector. The actual managed
port's standing also withholds Grok discovery; no renderer-supplied claim of
standalone or an omitted environment ceiling can qualify it. The detector retains
its own managed gate as well.

Results distinguish `discovered`, `configured-fallback` and `unavailable`.
Configured values survive failed discovery and catalog omissions. The current
Grok detector uses `model` only to append a fallback, so the handler removes it
from the probe-call copy and preserves it in the response instead: a failed
one-item fallback cannot be mistaken for a native catalog. A nonempty native
catalog supports `discovered`; empty results conservatively remain unavailable.
This is catalog provenance, never a claim that every appended configured value is
valid. Effort/config metadata remains unavailable until acknowledged per-model
support lands. No raw diagnostics, credentials, full environment or cache hashes
are returned or logged by this handler. Unexpected errors are reported through a
fresh fixed error and fixed response reason. `configGeneration` stays absent.

Ambiguous legacy Grok string calls reject with a fixed context-required error,
including legacy SSH calls; they never probe the host account. A blank unsaved
provider executable cannot override the detector's stored custom executable, so
that preview returns `executable-context-unavailable` until the setting is saved.
Nonblank custom executables and per-agent inheritance are preserved.

### Remaining owners and validation scope

GT1-02-05B1 owns the renderer's manually declared API overloads, and C/D/E own
request construction and stale-response guards in consumers. G owns IPC/preload
coverage and GT1-02-13 owns execution/type checking. Regression coverage should
include missing/mismatched saved agents, draft and unsaved provider settings,
empty tab selections, two accounts, `{}` replacement, global blanks, explicit
custom binaries, malformed falsy SSH inputs, unavailable/disabled/conflicting
remotes, managed refusal, configured-only versus native catalogs, empty catalogs,
fixed sanitized diagnostics and unchanged non-Grok positional preload calls.

GT1-03-10A/B retain per-model metadata and scoped remote discovery; phase09 retains
managed qualification. GT1-02-05A1/A2 retain the inherited-pipe descendant and
Windows helper deadline repairs. No strict end-to-end probe completion, remote
catalog, native version compatibility or tier-1 acceptance is claimed here.
P03/P06/REG-MODEL/REG-DISCOVERY bookkeeping names the implemented IPC boundary while
preserving every acceptance/applicability status. Tests, collection, compiler
checks and native discovery remain **not-run** under this IMPLEMENT packet.

## GT1-02-05A1 - Retained descendant ownership through pipe close

Date: 2026-10-30. Source: pre-task HEAD
`e98322c9dbc1079fca2e3ec30d384ef74d870e4a` plus this task's local diff.
Execution root and branch match CONTRACT. The observed base
`feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc` is
`882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`; `git rev-list --left-right --count
feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc...HEAD` reports **10 base-only / 29 task-only**
commits. Base ancestry check exits **1**, consistent with the recorded diverged
worktree. No switch, rebase, fetch, merge or primary checkout change.

### Implementation and limits

- `run/start-turn.ts`: add optional `trackDescendants` to the existing turn
  options. It observes owned POSIX processes from spawn through pipe close;
  ordinary callers retain their spawn and tracking behavior. Stops after parent
  exit can still sweep verified descendants. Completion still drains stdout and
  stderr on `close`, joins a pending ownership observation/sweep and releases the
  admission once. A stop after settlement is inert. An opted-in process that
  emits a post-spawn signal error retains ownership and uses the stop ladder
  instead of being released as though it never started.
- `control/termination.ts`: reuse canonical snapshot/refresh/survivor operations
  behind a child-keyed observation record. Before a stop, synchronously refresh
  verified roots to preserve children created since the last interval. Merge
  late async observations without dropping newer synchronous entries, and sweep
  late discoveries after a requested stop. The record survives parent exit;
  close/error settlement clears its timer and joins its last read. Root and
  survivor identities are checked again for opted-in final kills, with the
  child's own handle retained as fallback. Untracked group/tree behavior and
  explicit descendant exclusion stay intact. Asynchronous `taskkill` now uses
  the existing **5,000 ms** helper timeout and its existing failure fallback.
- `control/process-tree.ts`: record the original root's PID/start time and
  preserve it across merges. A reused root cannot seed traversal merely because
  its PPID is again this host. Legacy rootless records can only follow recorded
  descendants. The existing async `ps` helper now has a **2,000 ms** timeout.
  Linux continues to read procfs and Windows does not fabricate POSIX ownership.

These are observation and cleanup primitives, not a universal containment
boundary. Snapshot polling can miss a wrapper that forks and exits between reads,
and Windows cannot recover an exited root's descendants using `taskkill /t`.
Dedicated process groups alone are also insufficient: a tool can leave the group,
and a group number can later be reused. No detached spawn, parallel runner,
Promise timeout race, artificial pipe destruction or early successful completion
was added. Strong lifetime guarantees and platform qualification remain **not-run**.

The named three-file packet cannot also change the fourth-file probe consumer or
add a fourth lifecycle suite. Under CONTRACT, **GT1-02-05A1B** now owns probe
wiring, the existing lifecycle guide entries, and explicit pre-spawn refusal for
strict Grok deadlines where proven host containment is unavailable. It must not
turn this observation option into an unsupported guarantee. **GT1-02-05A3** owns
the existing process-tree suite; **GT1-02-05A2** retains the other three lifecycle
suites with the detailed playbook handoff. **GT1-02-05G/06** retain consumer and
detector coverage, and **GT1-02-13** executes all of them. No source or test work
from those continuation tasks was performed here.

### Review and verification

Independent static review: **pass** for the observed-descendant foundation after
preserving the untracked final-kill path, retaining ownership on post-spawn
errors, and reconciling synchronous/async snapshot races. This is source review,
not behavior certification. Test authoring, collection/execution, TypeScript
checking and native provider calls are **not-run** under this IMPLEMENT packet.
P03/P06/REG-MODEL/REG-DISCOVERY retain all acceptance/applicability statuses and
record the remaining owners and containment blocker. No capability is promoted.

Images analyzed: **0**. Local commit only under [[CONTRACT]], with the established
command-local hooks override for the missing/unpinned lint-staged hook. Preserve
and exclude `.sloptimize/`. The shared playbook is updated in place outside the
checkout and is not included in this repository commit. See [[GROK-TIER1-02]],
[[GROK-TIER1-03]] and [[Plans/grok-tier1/DESIGN]].

  `docs/agent-guides/CANONICAL-UTILITIES.md` and the shared lifecycle guidance in
  `docs/agent-guides/PROCESS-SYSTEM.md`. They describe opt-in observation,
  PID/start-time identity, normal stream draining, joined observations,
  exactly-once release, helper timeouts and platform limits.

Read-only source audit found **no proven host lifetime containment mechanism**
on this probe route. `startTurn` spawns a pipe-backed child and settles on
`close`. `trackProcessTree` retains only observed POSIX descendants and has no
Windows tracker; a rapid fork/exit can evade its snapshots. Process groups are
not a containment claim, and `taskkill /t` cannot recover an exited root's orphan.
The finite taskkill/process-table helper timeouts do not bound all descendants.
No new runner, native addon, detached process-group scheme, timeout race or
artificial pipe destruction was introduced. Grok native discovery availability
and containment qualification remain **not-run** and required blockers.

model retention with no unscoped native cache read or snapshot publication.
Exercise the output/deadline-trigger and managed release paths with eligible
non-Grok controlled fixtures: Grok now refuses before those paths. Test opt-in
tracking only on controlled calls, unchanged legacy standalone/managed calls,
cancellation during admission, and sink/spawn failures. A2/A3 retain lifecycle

## GT1-02-05F1 group-chat unknown context observations

Date: 2026-10-30. Pre-task HEAD: `ba8c0064f288219a25e4ba3d20e656a9b1a7c4ef`. Execution root and
branch match [[CONTRACT]] and `run-state.json`. Observed base
`feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc`: `882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`; merge base
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`. The local ancestry command reported
**10 base-only / 45 task-only** commits. No fetch, branch change or base update.

Implementation: **implemented** for the single named production file,
`src/shared/maestro-lib/groupchat/router.ts`:

- Resolve a missing/nonpositive/non-finite usage window from the process's stored
  participant `agentId` or moderator `moderatorAgentId`. Reuse
  `getContextWindowForAgent` for Grok's unknown result; preserve the existing 200K
  group-chat fallback for ordinary providers, including those with different
  provider defaults. Positive finite supplied windows bypass provider lookup.
- Require a positive denominator before computing a percentage, including empty
  usage. Unknown participant windows explicitly remove old context/token display
  fields; unknown moderator observations carry `-2`, distinct from accumulated
  `-1` observations that preserve the last valid measurement. Costs still advance.
  These display fields are separate from the token/cost accounting ledger.
- Record ledger usage synchronously before any asynchronous operation. Reuse
  `createKeyedWriteQueue` to order provider reads and card writes per chat, including
  replacement participant turns and moderator synthesis. Rejections release the
  queue; deleted chats remain contained and unexpected errors reach captureException.
- Expose `drainUsageUpdates()` over the queue's existing idle barrier. No new
  provider registry, context metadata, model inference, spawner or storage exists.

### Coverage handoff and remaining limits

Static inspection found the renderer currently treats every negative moderator
value as preserve-previous, and `ParticipantCard` renders absent context as 0%.
The engine's unknown observation does not fix those consumers on its own.
Bounded GT1-02-05F1A implements the numeric contract documentation, renderer handler
and shared participant/moderator card; GT1-02-05F1AT authors their tests.

The headless runtime currently drains only its store. An asynchronous provider
lookup can enqueue a write after that barrier; bounded GT1-02-05F1B must await the
engine barrier first, with GT1-02-05F1BT owning its isolated runtime tests. This
packet does not claim completed renderer or shutdown behavior.

GT1-02-05F2 authors observation tests, including awaited moderator events (two
existing assertions assume synchronous events), ordering across known/unknown
windows and replacement turns, immediate ledger finishing, rejection recovery,
and draining before fixture teardown. GT1-02-13 executes all affected suites.
Phase07 GT1-07-02/04/11 retain upstream unknown-window propagation and qualified
positive overrides. Phase03/11 retain acknowledged model/account and native proof.

P03/P06/P31/REG-MODEL/REG-DISCOVERY blockers and owner tasks record these bounded
handoffs; acceptance/applicability statuses remain unchanged. Tests, collection,
TypeScript, UI execution and real-provider/platform qualification are **not-run**
under this IMPLEMENT packet. No native calls or credential reads occurred. Images
analyzed: **0**. See [[GROK-TIER1-02]] and [[GROK-TIER1-03]].

Related contract: [[Plans/grok-tier1/DESIGN]] model/effort discovery, with native
acknowledgement owned by [[GROK-TIER1-03]].

Pre-task source HEAD: `97d437064138811fd7367310c129f84d5b5ef8d6`.
Root and branch matched `run-state.json` and [[CONTRACT]]. Observed base
`feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc` was
`882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`; merge base remained
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`, with **54 task-only / 10 base-only**
commits. No fetch, branch change or base update. Initial tracked tree was clean;
unrelated `.sloptimize/` remains excluded.

### Native contract and implementation

The pinned [native model-state implementation](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/xai-org/grok-build/2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8/crates/codegen/xai-grok-pager/src/acp/model_state.rs)
was read without running Grok: HTTP 200, SHA-256
`7631ec6886e1f42c4fca4a7c5400a5ed1d542366f1efdee4a275ac85132a03ec`.
Raw lines 11-17 and 287-325 validate a canonical wire value or a case-insensitive
menu ID against the selected model. Advertised IDs can be arbitrary strings and
can map to different wire values; they are not a universal canonical effort list.
The legacy menu fixture at line 543 contains xhigh/high/medium/low, demonstrating
that even minimal is not universally valid. Cached phase01 `headless.rs:693-780`
provides the CLI error prefix and can silently ignore an effort when the catalog
or model capability is absent. Argument emission alone is not native acceptance.

- `src/shared/modelTiers.ts` adds stable Grok candidate positions
  **minimal / medium / max** across the non-none vocabulary, using the lower
  middle of six rungs. Resolution requires an explicit list of advertised wire
  values for the current model/account/host/version and exact membership of the
  candidate. Missing evidence, a missing rung or a remembered refusal returns
  `undefined`; menu reordering/additions never remap a hint. No Grok model-tier
  table is added, so configured model routing remains authoritative.
- `src/shared/effortRejection.ts` recognizes the native prefixed selected-model
  refusal and empty-menu variant. Accepted IDs remain diagnostic strings; an
  ambiguous escaped diagnostic does not lose the rejected rung. No model is
  invented from a message that names none. Generic ACP errors, ignored-effort
  warnings and a missing catalog do not become guessed model rejections.
  Grok memory uses native ASCII-insensitive matching; other providers keep their
  previous key behavior and parsers.
- `src/shared/maestro-lib/providers/definitions.ts` retains manual text and an
  empty default. Its existing builder refuses remembered rejected values rather
  than silently dropping them, and conservatively refuses explicit `none` because
  it currently receives no selected-model proof. Other explicit values retain
  the single trimmed `--reasoning-effort` pair and native validation path.
  No models, capabilities, public menu or version range are promoted.

### Bounded remaining work and test handoff

Current hint consumers do not pass advertised wire values, so Grok hints still
inherit through the existing warning path. The new optional resolver input is a
safe boundary for later scoped producers, not an assertion of working discovery.
Explicit `none` is unavailable through the configuration builder even on a model
that might support it; the task deliberately refuses rather than guesses.

Six executable continuations were inserted before GT1-02-08 without rewriting
existing task lines:

- GT1-02-07A/07AT: canonical final-argument composition, selected-model validation,
  effort/model custom-argument conflicts and both native effort aliases. Custom
  arguments currently bypass the configuration builder's guards; no end-to-end
  no-resend or none guarantee is claimed for that path.
- GT1-02-07B/07BT: optional evidence through shared hint joins, inspection/replay
  and cheap-turn resolution, with precise missing-menu/missing-rung reporting.
- GT1-02-07C/07CT: existing CLI/renderer/groomer retry paths must preserve explicit
  choices and refuse an automatic retry when provenance or a distinct validated
  fallback is unknown. Clearing a custom effort can otherwise restore the same
  provider/session setting. Matching a ladder word alone is not provenance.

GT1-02-08 owns core regression authoring for every canonical rung, all candidate
positions, exact membership, legacy/missing menus, unsupported model tiers,
raw/JSON native diagnostics, arbitrary offered IDs, ASCII case matching,
remembered refusals and the configuration builder. GT1-02-13 executes all named
suites and applicable type checks. GT1-03-10A/10B owns acknowledged native menu
production, alias-to-wire mapping and model-change invalidation; phase08's
workflow/utility consumers must propagate scoped observations. None of those
native or consumer paths is certified by this packet.

### Implementation and evidence boundary

Re-read the pinned [native model-state contract](https://raw.githubusercontent.com/xai-org/grok-build/2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8/crates/codegen/xai-grok-pager/src/acp/model_state.rs)
and phase01's cached `headless.rs:693-780`. The wire vocabulary is separate from
arbitrary selector IDs; emitting flags is not native acknowledgement. No Grok
binary, authentication, discovery process or paid request was run.

- `launch/agent-args.ts` now owns `resolveGrokModelEffortArgs`. It reads the final
  model and both effort aliases in split/equals forms, trims values, consolidates
  equivalent repeated selectors and refuses conflicts or missing values. Model
  IDs stay exact; effort comparison and remembered rejection use native ASCII
  case semantics. Generic flag deduplication preserves Grok selection pairs so
  it cannot hide conflicting values or create orphan arguments.
- `applyAgentConfigOverrides` validates after provider/session settings and
  custom arguments are joined. The existing custom tokenizer handles quoted
  assignment values and rejects unmatched quotes for Grok only. Pre-prompt `--`
  and attached `-mVALUE` are explicitly refused rather than risking a hidden
  selection. Other providers retain their existing argument/tokenizer behavior.
- `GrokEffortMenu` reuses shared discovery status/model types and carries an
  explicit canonical wire-value list, never picker `options` (which include
  configured fallbacks). A discovered menu must match the final explicit model.
  Matching evidence requires exact advertised wire membership; arbitrary menu
  IDs cannot qualify as wire values. `none` needs matching evidence offering
  `none`. Missing, unavailable, fallback or stale-model evidence cannot authorize
  it. Explicit non-none selections still reach native validation without a
  matching menu; no model allowlist, substitute model or substitute rung is used.
- `launch/launch-plan.ts` revalidates direct callers after managed model pinning,
  before prompt/environment/secret delivery, and returns the typed
  `grok-model-effort-refused` result. Equals-form model flags no longer cause a
  second Grok managed pin. Local and resolved-SSH plans carry the validated args;
  existing managed/SSH authority checks remain in place.
- `providers/definitions.ts` emits the scalar effort pair. Its previous guard
  moved to final composition because the scalar callback cannot see the final
  model or later custom aliases. Static call-site audit found only
  `applyAgentConfigOverrides` invokes production configuration builders.

No current consumer supplies an acknowledged native menu. Host/account/version,
executable, workspace and target freshness, menu-ID mappings and native
acknowledgement remain with GT1-03-10A/10B and existing phase08 consumers. The new
optional input is a host-owned validation contract, not authentication of a
caller-supplied menu or certification of an operational discovery path. An
implicit native default cannot authorize `none` without a final explicit model.

aliases, preserved pre-dedupe conflicts, stale/unavailable menus, arbitrary IDs,

the scalar callback now only emits the requested value. GT1-02-13 owns all test
and type-check execution. No tests were authored or run in this IMPLEMENT packet.

merge base `0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`, with **64 task-only /

- `providers/definitions.ts`: added an explicit `promptFile` declaration to the
  canonical AgentConfig type, reusing the existing `promptFileArgs` builder.
  The builder alone retains OMP's legacy Windows attachment behavior. No provider
  opts in yet and no capability/public-support assertion changes.
- `launch/prompt-delivery.ts`: added the file and refusal variants. A mandatory
  file precedes SSH and Windows stdin routing and preserves the complete supplied
  text, including empty/whitespace-only strings. The host supplies a fresh,
  collision-resistant absolute destination for that executing host; planning
  does not allocate a name, read a file, create a file or consult randomness.
  The artifact requires UTF-8, mode 0600, exclusive `wx` creation and cleanup
  after its reader closes, including refusal, failed upload/spawn, cancellation,
  timeout and supersession. Collisions must refuse without deleting another
  owner's file. Path checks reuse canonical `isAbsolutePath`.
- The initial format is text with a `.txt` destination. Unsupported embedded
  images, missing builders/destinations, wrong local/remote ownership and
  conflicting prompt flags/separators return fixed diagnostics without prompt
  content or paths. Conflict checks include split, equals and attached-short
  aliases supplied by the provider declaration. Existing providers follow the
  untouched argv/stdin/SSH branches, including their existing empty-string rule.
- `launch/launch-plan.ts`: threads the destination and base args into the pure
  resolver, returns `prompt-delivery-refused` on failure and composes file args
  once on local or remote plans. Successful plans exclude the refusal variant;
  file delivery leaves raw stdin undefined. Creation and cleanup are obligations,
  not claimed side effects or verified lifecycle behavior.

## GT1-02-09A ordered native ACP JSON prompt artifacts

Implemented JSON byte planning in the three named production files. This is an
inactive delivery contract: Grok's definition and public capability flags are
unchanged, and no artifact is materialized or dispatched by this packet.
Related: [[GROK-TIER1-02]], [[CONTRACT]], [[Plans/grok-tier1/DESIGN]] and [[01]].

Pre-task HEAD: `daa8bdec1e995a0ab8f2422b1832426c3e405306`. Execution root and branch
match CONTRACT and `run-state.json`. Observed base `feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc`:
`882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`; merge base
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`, with **65 task-only / 10 base-only**
commits. Initial tracked tree clean; unrelated `.sloptimize/` preserved and
excluded. No fetch, branch changes or provider invocation. Images analyzed: **0**.

### Contract and reuse

DESIGN's [pinned native parser](https://github.com/xai-org/grok-build/blob/2bdd1d6a6369de0e8c68132ea4539e9abd9e14a8/crates/codegen/xai-grok-pager/src/headless/cli.rs#L53-L116)
interprets lowercase `.json` as ACP blocks, rejects an empty block array, and
trims other file extensions. A text block may itself be empty. The
[pinned ACP schema](https://docs.rs/agent-client-protocol-schema/0.11.4/src/agent_client_protocol_schema/content.rs.html)
uses `type: image`, `data` and `mimeType`; it supplies no universal MIME allowlist,
image count or byte limits. This implementation therefore requires explicit
caller admission bounds instead of inventing upstream limits or model support.
The inspected public source remains separate from the unqualified npm binary.

Reuse search covered the shared/process/agent guides, canonical launch entry,
`image-refs.ts`, `stream-json-message.ts`, CLI image reader, session image store
and renderer image utilities. The existing data-URL parser may resolve a host
image ref with I/O. Claude's serializer reorders images before text and skips
invalid input; its envelope is not ACP. The CLI reader requires filesystem
access. No reusable pure ACP image validator was found, so the bounded validator
and encoder live in the existing shared image-reference module. Legacy parsers
and serialization paths remain unchanged.

### Implementation and consumer handoff

- `providers/definitions.ts` permits the opt-in `acp-json` format. It does not
  select that format for any provider or assert image capability.
- `launch/image-refs.ts` exposes ordered `AcpPromptBlock` values and
  `EmbeddedImageSupport` for the executing host's admitted route/model/turn.
  `buildAcpPromptFileContent` projects only text/image wire fields into
  `{"type":"acp","content":[...]}`. It preserves exact string values and block
  order, including empty/whitespace-only text and multiline Unicode. Empty or
  malformed block lists, unknown kinds, path/resource blocks, missing support,
  unsupported MIME, invalid base64, and exceeded count/individual/total byte
  bounds return fixed diagnostics with no content or paths. Byte bounds precede
  decoding; a canonical base64 round trip rejects Node's permissive decoding of
  malformed input. Limits must be positive safe integers. No filesystem reads,
  reference resolution, tool grant, permission change or retry occurs.
- `launch/prompt-delivery.ts` accepts either prompt text or resolved ordered
  blocks, never both. JSON requires a lowercase `.json` executing-host destination;
  the existing exclusive-source flag checks and owned UTF-8/0600/`wx`/cleanup
  contract are retained. Blocks on an unsupported route and `hasImages` without
  actual embedded bytes refuse, including image-only requests. Null block input
  reaches validation instead of becoming empty text. Mandatory file refusal
  does not retry via raw stdin, argv text, or a tool-read image path.

GT1-02-10 must thread `promptBlocks` and `embeddedImageSupport` through
`launch-plan.ts` and its consumers before image activation: the existing launch
input forwards only `hasImages`. Hosts must resolve authorized attachment refs
before planning, bind support/bounds to the selected route/model and fresh or
resumed identity, preserve ordering and composed instructions, and materialize
only on the executing host. The existing desktop/pipe/SSH prompt and image
assembly must consume the artifact without adding a second prompt or tool-read
fallback. Missing route/model evidence stays a refusal. No-tools/read-only
authority is independent of embedded-image support and must never be widened.
These are already owned by GT1-02-10 and phase07's GT1-07-03D/03E; split bounded
consumer continuations under CONTRACT if necessary before GT1-02-11 activation.

GT1-02-11 must declare all four native prompt aliases (`-p`, `--single`,
`--prompt-json`, `--prompt-file`) in `exclusivePromptFlags` and emit exactly one
`--prompt-file` source. That grammar stays provider-owned, not hardcoded into
the shared ACP encoding. Every dispatched turn must supply canonical text or
blocks; the existing no-prompt interactive path is not native turn validation.
GT1-02-09B owns ordered/malformed/null blocks, strict MIME/base64/byte/count
bounds, omitted support, exact text, fresh/resumed input and conflicting flags;
GT1-02-12 owns launch propagation/artifact cleanup, and GT1-02-13 executes checks.

Updated gap descriptions only for P07, P08, P39, C-supportsImageInput,
C-supportsImageInputOnResume, C-supportsPromptViaStdin and REG-DEFINITIONS.
Acceptance states, owners, required flags, applicability and live authorization
remain unchanged. This packet is implemented; deterministic and live evidence
remain **not-run**.

`882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`; merge base
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`, with **67 task-only / 10 base-only**

### Local implementation and reuse

- `src/shared/maestro-lib/run/start-turn.ts`: `turnProcessSpecFromPlan` carries
  a private artifact without I/O. The existing sink validates local ownership,
  absolute path, UTF-8/0600/exclusive/turn-cleanup metadata and absent stdin.
  It retains the descriptor immediately after exclusive creation, writes exact
  bytes through that descriptor, and removes only the matching inode after the
  reader closes or preparation/spawn refuses/fails. A collision never conveys
  ownership. A replaced path is retained; missing files are harmless. Cleanup
  I/O failures produce fixed warnings without paths or content, and descriptor
  close is attempted even when cleanup fails. This is normal process-lifetime
  cleanup, not crash recovery or a guarantee against OS cleanup failures.
- The sink copies artifact, arguments and environment before admission callbacks
  can mutate the caller's originals. Artifact launches check an unbound lease
  before any write and again immediately before spawn; ordinary launches retain
  one check. Pre-aborted artifact launches write/spawn nothing. A final refusal
  or abort removes owned preparation. Parser construction now precedes file and
  child ownership. An error signalling a started artifact reader requests stop
  while retaining ownership until close rather than treating it as spawn failure.
- `src/shared/maestro-lib/managed/authority.ts`: optional
  `promptArtifactDigest` hashes the canonical complete artifact, binding content,
  destination, host and creation/cleanup metadata without exposing content in
  the snapshot. Specs without artifacts retain their previous snapshot shape.
  This does not qualify Grok's managed route or tool/effect containment.
- Updated the existing start-turn entry in
  `docs/agent-guides/CANONICAL-UTILITIES.md`. Searched the process/agent/shared
  guides and source before extending the canonical owner. Existing legacy
  `removeTempFiles` is asynchronous and logs paths; it cannot supply this sink's
  descriptor-verified, completion-ordered private cleanup. No new standalone
  helper, spawner, registry or provider capability was introduced.

An independent audit identified the remaining concrete consumers: launch-plan
and pipe planning (10B); SSH builder, remote signal and guard (10C); shared SSH
wrapper/chat/group-chat (10D); desktop request/artifact binding (10E0), IPC and child spawner (10E); CLI and Cue
spec conversion (10F); context grooming/runtime background turns (10G). Existing
10A/10AT own refusal classification. The SSH builder currently logs script
previews, and its legacy image cleanup neither traps cancellation nor preserves
provider exit status. The remote PID helper assumes `exec`; a supervising
artifact script must preserve process-group identity and cleanup after hard
stop before activation. A trap alone cannot handle SIGKILL. None of those remote
or consumer gaps is claimed fixed by this packet. A follow-up handoff review
found that desktop ProcessConfig cannot yet carry the artifact or bind it in
request admission; 10E0/10E0T explicitly own that prerequisite before 10E.

## GT1-02-10B launch and pipe prompt artifact handoff

Implementation: **implemented** in the three named production files. Runtime,
platform and native qualification remain **not-run**. Related: [[GROK-TIER1-02]],
[[CONTRACT]], [[Plans/grok-tier1/DESIGN]] and [[01]].

Pre-task HEAD: `77f8d541ddbcb0e2868cff97c64c90b88423feb3`. Execution root and branch
match CONTRACT and `run-state.json`. Observed base `feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc`:
`882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`; merge base
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`, with **69 task-only / 10 base-only**
commits. Initial tracked tree clean; unrelated `.sloptimize/` preserved and
excluded. No fetch, branch change or provider invocation. Images analyzed: **0**.

- `prompt-delivery.ts`: `selectPromptFileDestination` chooses a fresh local UUID
  name for each launch attempt without creating a file. It preserves an explicitly
  supplied host destination and never allocates a local path for a resolved remote.
  The existing deterministic resolver and ACP encoder remain separate from name
  selection. Remote launch plans require a POSIX absolute destination whose
  `sshRemoteId` matches the selected SSH configuration; missing, mismatched or
  local-owned remote destinations refuse. The remote owner must still obtain the
  actual path on that host; a configuration ID is not native endpoint qualification.
- `launch-plan.ts`: carries ordered `promptBlocks` and `embeddedImageSupport`
  through the canonical resolver, preserving its exact empty/Unicode text,
  image-admission validation and fixed refusals. Composed text stays ordered;
  staged local translation knowledge binds once to the first nonempty text block
  using the existing binder. Image-only content requiring that knowledge refuses
  with `prompt-delivery-refused` unless the caller supplies composed text. This
  prevents silently dropping instructions without inserting synthetic user text.
- `pipe-spawn.ts`: accepts the canonical local file delivery in `promptDelivery`.
  Its `args` must already contain the complete launch-plan arguments, including
  the file argument tail. The pipe validates that tail and does not append it
  again. File delivery precedes legacy prompt/image processing, bypasses raw
  stdin flags and the Windows OMP attachment writer, and copies the private
  artifact to `spec.promptArtifact`. It creates no prompt/image files and puts
  no artifact in `tempFiles`; `startTurn` retains exclusive creation and cleanup.
  Even empty file prompts are batch turns with closed stdin. An opted-in provider
  with prompt/images but no file plan refuses instead of silently falling back.
  Remote artifacts must pass through the SSH owner before local pipe planning.

No provider declaration or capability was activated. Existing OMP/Claude/Codex
branches remain intact when explicit file delivery is absent. Remote allocation,
transfer and hard-stop cleanup remain GT1-02-10C; caller handoffs remain
GT1-02-10D/10E0/10E/10F/10G. Those consumers must carry the plan without reappending
its arguments, resolve/admit attachments before planning, and supply composed
text when staged knowledge accompanies image-only input. GT1-02-10BT/12 author
regressions, including the knowledge refusal and selected-remote identity;
GT1-02-13 owns execution. No additional implementation task was needed.

Updated only the seven existing parity gap descriptions for P07, P08, P39,
C-supportsImageInput, C-supportsImageInputOnResume, C-supportsPromptViaStdin and
REG-DEFINITIONS. Acceptance statuses, applicability, source/owner lists,
required flags and live authorization remain unchanged.

## GT1-02-10C SSH prompt artifact preparation and lifetime

Implemented the inactive remote artifact path in the three named production
files. Runtime, platform and native qualification remain **not-run**. Related:
[[GROK-TIER1-02]], [[CONTRACT]], [[Plans/grok-tier1/DESIGN]] and [[01]].

Pre-task HEAD: `2ce2386e3e1bdd16c99b456ba0a7c633b1ab497b`. Execution root and branch
match CONTRACT and `run-state.json`. Observed base `feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc`:
`882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`; merge base
`0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`, with **70 task-only / 10 base-only**
commits. Initial tracked tree clean; unrelated `.sloptimize/` preserved and
excluded. No fetch, branch change or provider invocation. Images analyzed: **0**.

- `ssh-command-builder.ts`: selects fresh UUID destinations in the supported
  remote Linux `/tmp` namespace without consulting local temp/home paths. The
  builder validates host identity, path and artifact metadata, snapshots private
  bytes before asynchronous work, and refuses separate prompt/stdin/image sources.
  Its explicit file branch precedes all legacy image/prompt assembly. Both SSH
  entry points force the stdin script for artifacts. A complete parsed compound
  carries base64 bytes, verifies SHA-256 before launch, creates with noclobber and
  a private umask, verifies mode 0600, and retains descriptor 9 for inode-checked
  cleanup. The provider receives closed stdin and no ownership descriptors;
  cleanup preserves its exit code. The executable uses remote-aware quoting.
  Removed script previews; private artifact argv is withheld from display text.
- `ssh-remote-signal.ts`: the optional artifact owner requires Linux procfs and
  a process-group-leading supervisor before preparation. It retains an exclusive
  private PID record on descriptor 8 with PID/start-time/group checks. Artifact
  records use a distinct namespace so the legacy age sweep cannot remove an
  active owner. Stop first publishes a private zero-byte cancellation tombstone;
  launch checks it before and after recording ownership, preventing delayed
  startup after a stop that arrived before the PID record. The signal script is
  also a complete parsed compound. It retains inode witnesses before TERM,
  observes the owned supervisor stopped before final descriptor capture and
  SIGKILL, and waits a bounded interval for group readers before cleanup. Zombies
  do not count as readers. Interrupted stop helpers thaw only the same recorded
  process identity and retain their witness through final reader checks and
  cleanup before releasing descriptors. No collision or replaced destination
  is deleted.
  Failed SSH signaling produces a fixed warning rather than a success log or
  raw exception content.
- `turn-guard.ts`: carries the private remote path through recording and stop
  metadata, refuses missing script routes, retains interrupt escalation after
  local SSH close, and requests remote termination for interrupted/failed
  transport completion, including post-spawn errors and managed stops. Local process settlement is
  not treated as proof that the remote reader exited.

The existing shell escaping, SSH options, PID/stop owner and guard were extended;
no second SSH spawner, polling service or provider declaration was added.
Only seven existing parity gap descriptions changed. Acceptance statuses,
applicability, source/owner lists, required flags and live authorization remain
unchanged. This opt-in path does not activate Grok prompt-file delivery.

### Consumer and qualification handoff

GT1-02-10D/10E/10F/10G must select the remote destination before canonical
planning, pass the exact artifact to the builder, and thread its path through
`recordRemoteAgent` or the desktop/Cue PID and signal owners. Direct owners use
`newRemotePidFilePath(true)` and the optional artifact path in both recording
and signaling. They must suppress previously appended raw prompts, stdin and
legacy images, retain the original admitted artifact for request binding, and
not forward a remote artifact to the local materializer. Builder argv must be
the canonical complete file plan; validation of competing native flags stays
with the planner. Missing owner binding refuses before artifact creation.

GT1-02-10CT/13 own deterministic coverage and execution for complete/truncated
transfers, empty/large/Unicode bytes, checksum and creation failures, collisions
and replacement, modes, custom executable quoting, normal/nonzero/missing
provider exits, pre-record cancellation, SIGINT/TERM/KILL, PID reuse, stopped
state observation, zombie readers, early local SSH close, managed stop and
failure diagnostics. No suite, collection, type check or shell harness ran here.

Remote macOS/Windows and Linux hosts without the required procfs, ownership or
utilities remain unavailable on this artifact route. A disconnected/unreachable
host, SIGKILL of the cleanup helper, lost PID/descriptor ownership or remaining
live readers yields unqualified cleanup, never a success assertion. Process-group observation does not certify
escaped descendants, crash recovery or remote containment. These routes remain
blocked before activation and require native lifecycle evidence in phase 11.
Cancellation tombstones intentionally persist: deleting one while launch is
delayed could re-enable that attempt. They contain no prompt bytes and are not
claimed cleaned. No automatic age-based deletion is introduced.

- `src/__tests__/shared/maestro-lib/run/start-turn.test.ts`: **23 cases** reuse
  `createScratchDir`, the existing EventEmitter/PassThrough child pattern and
  scoped mutable module facades. Real filesystem assertions cover exact empty,
  large and Unicode JSON bytes; exclusive creation and POSIX 0600 mode; collision,
  replacement and missing-path preservation; partial writes; synchronous and
  asynchronous spawn failures; parser-construction failure; success/nonzero exit;
  cancellation before, during and after admission; and post-spawn signal errors.
  The file and retained descriptor survive reader exit until pipe close. Every
  acquired-descriptor cleanup path asserts one close and EBADF; injected cleanup
  I/O failures exercise the close attempt and fixed no-content warnings. A close
  error is simulated after releasing the real descriptor; this is not an OS
  guarantee that a failed close releases a handle. Teardown tracks descriptor
  ownership rather than closing a potentially reused descriptor a second time.
  Preflight denial writes nothing, final denial removes owned preparation, and
  callbacks cannot replace admitted content, destination, argv or environment.
- `src/__tests__/shared/maestro-lib/managed/authority.test.ts`: **12 cases** reuse
  the existing spec, authority and admission fixtures. Changes to exact bytes
  (empty, whitespace and Unicode normalization), destination, owner, SSH target,
  encoding, permissions, creation flag or cleanup metadata invalidate an admitted
  lease. Original input mutation cannot alter an existing snapshot. Private bytes
  and paths are excluded from snapshots. An absent artifact retains the legacy
  shape and independently derived fixed digest, distinct from an empty artifact.

Updated the existing blockers in P07, P08, P39, C-supportsImageInput,

Images analyzed: **0**.

- `cue-spawn-builder.ts` owns the issued plugin identity and proof file until
  injection construction, CLI-path resolution, argument merging and environment
  composition succeed. A failed or unused injection revokes the token and removes
  the owned file in one finally path. Preparation failures return a fixed build
  refusal without raw exceptions, prompt-bearing argv or private paths. Only a
  successful spec transfers ownership to the executor.
- `cue-executor.ts` protects successful specs immediately after the build await,
  before the cancellation check, logging, heartbeat or managed-admission setup.
  The existing finally now covers post-build cancellation and setup exceptions,
  as well as process completion, refusal and failure. Failed builds remain owned
  by the builder; cancellation retains precedence over a failed build. Cleanup
  warnings report removal failures without raw filesystem errors. Proof cleanup
  still precedes transcript callbacks.

Reused `pluginToolRunIdentity.revoke` and `removePluginRunProofFile`; no helper,
writer, spawner, retry or plugin subsystem was added. Prompt artifact I/O and
cleanup remain with the shared local sink or SSH lifecycle owner. Normal
completion/nonzero exit and managed-refusal result handling are unchanged.

GT1-02-10F1T owns regression authoring in the two existing Cue suites; GT1-02-13
owns test execution and type checks. Include creation/injection/argument-merge

## GT1-02-10G1 desktop pending-admission cancellation

Closed the implementation gap identified by GT1-02-10G in the three named
production files. Behavioral and native evidence remain **not-run**.
Related: [[GROK-TIER1-02]], [[CONTRACT]], [[Plans/grok-tier1/DESIGN]] and [[01]].

Pre-task HEAD: `998b55f06b54d9d45431086179d6b7ea98b7aa90`. Execution root and
branch match CONTRACT and `run-state.json`. Observed base
`feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc`: `882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`;
merge base `0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`, with **91 task-only /
10 base-only** commits. Initial tracked tree clean; unrelated `.sloptimize/`
preserved and excluded. No fetch, branch change or provider invocation.
Images analyzed: **0**.

- `types.ts` defines optional internal `ProcessSpawnOptions.signal`, passed
  separately from `ProcessConfig`. No signal or abort reason enters a launch
  request, managed content digest, process capture or serialization.
- `ProcessManager.spawnAdmitted` checks cancellation before asking admission and
  immediately after it settles, before standalone, admitted or refused handling.
  A granted but unused lease is removed from the manager's admission map and
  released through that lease with a fixed spawn-failed message. Cancellation
  returns `pid: -1`, `success: false` and fixed text, without policy-denial
  metadata or raw abort/authority errors; a canceled rejected decision uses the
  same result. Uncanceled failures retain their existing behavior. No local
  artifact materialization or remote script launch is reached after cancellation
  during admission. No new spawner, writer or provider activation was introduced.
- `context-groomer.ts` preserves its controller-backed cancellation callback
  through the admitted spawn, listens for abort to reject/stop and removes that
  listener during existing cleanup. It forwards the signal as internal options,
  preserving actor/utility lineage and the exact launch configuration. Active
  readers still stop through the existing manager; their bound leases and
  artifacts keep their existing lifetime owners. Legacy late-child handling is
  retained for alternate manager implementations.

Seven existing parity gap descriptions were updated. Requirement statuses,
applicability, required flags and live authorization are unchanged. Grok remains
beta; prompt-file activation and platform/remote cleanup qualification remain
with their existing owners. GT1-02-10G1T owns focused tests for pre-abort,
pending standalone/admitted/refused/rejected decisions, exactly-once unused
lease release, no local writes/SSH starts, fixed private diagnostics, unchanged
capture/digests and active-reader stop ownership. GT1-02-10GT owns broader
consumer tests and GT1-02-13 owns execution/type checks. No tests, collection,
type checks, native calls or paid operations were run in this IMPLEMENT packet.

### Discovered repair and remaining evidence

Independent static review found a concrete **fail** in selected remote cwd
propagation: `context-groomer.ts` builds provider arguments with `projectRoot`
before applying `sessionSshRemoteConfig.workingDirOverride` to the SSH shell.
Grok's existing `workingDirArgs` emits `--cwd` for that local path. A remote
shell `cd` does not override the provider's explicit argument. The two new
remote selection cases now require the selected remote cwd at canonical
argument construction; static inspection predicts those assertions fail until
**GT1-02-10G2** repairs the source. No test was executed to produce this finding.
The bounded implementation checkbox precedes activation and verification in
[[GROK-TIER1-02]]; no production fix is claimed by this TEST packet.

Seven parity blocker descriptions record authored coverage and the cwd defect;
their implementation owner lists now include GT1-02-10G2. Acceptance statuses,
required flags, applicability and live authorization remain unchanged. GT1-02-13
must execute these suites after that repair. Grok remains beta with prompt-file
activation withheld. Both public inputs are text-only: no image argument or
capability is added, and ordered-image admission stays with GT1-02-09B/10BT/
10DT/10D1T/12 and later native qualification. Existing Grok no-tools absence and
managed binding limits remain explicit refusals, not simulated support.

## GT1-02-11 Grok prompt-file selection

Selected Grok's owned ACP JSON prompt-file route in the canonical registry and
removed its unverified raw-stdin assertion. Only the two named provider files
changed; `src/main/utils/system-prompt-delivery.ts` already delegates to the
shared implementation and needs no duplicate branch. Related:
[[GROK-TIER1-02]], [[CONTRACT]], [[Plans/grok-tier1/DESIGN]] and [[01]].

branch match CONTRACT and `run-state.json`. Observed base
`feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc`: `882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`;
merge base `0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`, with **96 task-only /
10 base-only** commits. Initial tracked tree clean; unrelated `.sloptimize/`
preserved and excluded. No fetch, branch change or provider invocation.
Images analyzed: **0**.

- `definitions.ts` selects `promptFile.format: 'acp-json'` and the existing
  file-argument seam emits `--prompt-file <owned-path>`. Its exclusive aliases
  are `-p`, `--single`, `--prompt-json` and `--prompt-file`. The shared resolver
  rejects competing aliases and `--` before adding the file flag once.
  Lowercase `.json` text blocks preserve whitespace where native plain text
  input would trim it. Host selection, exclusive creation, remote ownership,
  closed stdin and reader cleanup reuse the preceding 09/10-series work.
- `capabilities.ts` sets `supportsPromptViaStdin: false` and retains
  `supportsAppendSystemPrompt: false`. Pinned-source rules/append syntax is a
  candidate, not verified absence or qualified support. Shared system delivery
  embeds the canonical envelope on the first user turn and skips it on resume;
  no native rules flag or second system-prompt implementation was added.
- The legacy `promptArgs` and image path builder remain declarations for their
  existing direct callers. Mandatory file consumers bypass them and refuse
  missing image bytes/admission. This change does not certify embedded images,
  expand permissions, enable ACP transport, or promote Grok from beta.

### Remaining consumer and coverage

Independent static review found `src/main/ipc/handlers/tabNaming.ts` still
appends raw `promptArgs(fullPrompt)` and submits `ProcessConfig` without an
artifact. The explicit-file guard in `ChildProcessSpawner.ts` consequently
refuses Grok naming turns after this registry selection. This is a known
functional gap with safe refusal, not completed naming support. Added bounded
**GT1-02-11C IMPLEMENT / GT1-02-11D TEST** before dependent tests/verification;
existing phase08 effort-retry ownership remains separate. No sink guard was
weakened and no broader consumer work was folded into this two-file packet.

GT1-02-12 now explicitly owns real-registry composition assertions and stale
Grok argv/SSH expectations in `launch-plan.test.ts`, including the remote-owned
JSON destination. Existing 10-series artifact suites remain in GT1-02-13's
execution scope. Five corresponding parity rows record this selection, naming
blocker and continuation ownership; acceptance statuses, required flags,
applicability and live authorization remain unchanged. Deterministic and
native evidence remain **not-run**, including exact-version rules semantics,
Windows permissions and remote cleanup. No supported version range is claimed.

Added a structured refusal in the shared launch planner for explicit Grok
additional-directory requests. Only the three named production files changed.
