branch match CONTRACT and `run-state.json`. Observed base
`feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc`: `882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`;
merge base `0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`, with **97 task-only /
10 base-only** commits. Tracked tree initially clean; unrelated `.sloptimize/`
is preserved and excluded. No branch change, fetch or native invocation.
Images analyzed: **0**.

### Evidence and implementation boundary

Phase01 does not establish a native CLI root-grant mechanism. Cached pinned
source `session_setup.rs:15-16,2002-2003` explicitly rejects nonempty ACP resume
`additionalDirectories` (SHA-256
`266eaf035f690ec57bf62b7d9f06bbf72922b17e07f6cc6229820a015cf05ea7`, recorded in
[[01]]). The same audit's `14-headless-mode.md:38-45,84-100` describes
`--allow`/`--deny` tool approval rules and a `--sandbox` profile, not additional
filesystem roots (SHA-256
`248d83a99ec6fbeb0443754b98cc195b3ab44168bf30a2f110e3188d4045f230`). These are
source observations; correspondence to candidate binary 1.0.70 remains unknown.

- `launch-plan.ts` now accepts `additionalDirectories` and returns
  `grok-directory-grants-refused` for every active Grok read/write request.
  Writable requests under read-only inputs/managed ceilings receive an explicit
  refusal. The existing managed mode/argument guards remain authoritative.
  Cross-provider `--add-dir` and `--add-dir=...` arguments are refused even
  without typed grants; neither spelling is claimed as a Grok native flag.
- Canonical normalization and access filters preserve blank, inert and
  last-duplicate-wins row semantics without local home expansion, filesystem
  reads, path substitution or argument mutation. Empty requests keep existing
  cwd, account, resume and SSH planning. No image/prompt path creates a grant.
  Refusals precede prompt preparation and secret reads and contain fixed text,
  never supplied roots, prompt bytes or credential values.
- `capabilities.ts` records CLI read/writable roots as unverified, pinned-source
  ACP resume roots as unsupported, and no qualified version range in separate
  investigation metadata. Public `supportsAdditionalDirectories` stays false;
  `definitions.ts` retains no `additionalDirArgs`, preserving the registry's
  iff invariant. No writable-root mechanism or containment is certified.

### Pending consumer propagation and qualification

Existing desktop, CLI, Cue and shared chat consumers drop directory objects at
argument construction before planning, because Grok has no native builder.
This packet protects explicit planner inputs and incompatible custom arguments;
it does **not** complete session-grant refusal across those consumers. Added
bounded continuations **GT1-02-11A1/11A2** for forwarding the selected grants and
mapping the refusal without mislabeling it as SSH lookup failure. Separate
**11A1T/11A2T** own consumer tests, existing **11B** owns canonical planner and
registry coverage, and **GT1-02-13** owns execution. All remain executable tasks
before verification; none is claimed completed here.

P08, P35, C-supportsAdditionalDirectories and REG-DEFINITIONS retain required
blockers for the absent qualified native grant mechanism, pending consumer
propagation and deterministic/native proof. Their acceptance fields,
applicability and live authorization are unchanged. No binary version, sandbox
profile, cwd, tool approval rule or prompt hint certifies a grant. Existing
phase09/11 owners must establish actual allowed/denied roots, symlink containment
and per-platform behavior before support can be promoted. Native qualification
and deterministic tests remain **not-run**; the pinned-source ACP resume extension
is **unsupported**. No paid/network/provider calls were made.

`feature/mega-maestro-v1.1.0-rc`: `882d658a8cfb8c538d59eae3d8cb041724b9d1f5`;

10 base-only** commits. Initial tracked tree clean; unrelated `.sloptimize/`
preserved and excluded. Images analyzed: **0**.

- File turns reuse `buildAgentLaunchPlan`, its canonical ACP JSON encoder and
  local destination selection, plus `newRemotePromptFileDestination` and
  `buildSshCommand` for the selected SSH host. Exactly one planned file source
  reaches ProcessConfig; raw prompt, promptArgs and prompt stdin are suppressed.
  Competing aliases refuse through the planner; a remote result without its
  stdin preparation script refuses before process startup.
- Request, provider configuration, global environment, selected SSH object and
  configured executable are captured before asynchronous detection/preparation.
  The remote cwd override feeds both provider arguments and the SSH command.
  File turns use the effective utility provider's hint ladder and explicit utility
  model, preventing a triggering Claude model/effort from contaminating Grok.
  Canonical account layering retains selected session replacement, blank unsets,
  provider/global precedence and child delegation stamping.
- Artifact creation and deletion remain entirely with ChildProcessSpawner /
  startTurn or the existing remote script/PID owner. The handler carries the
  private artifact, selected host and script through the existing sink, retaining
  admission and cleanup guards. It neither writes files nor bypasses refusals.
  File turns require the existing enforced read-only declaration when no all-tools
  switch is available; no image or no-tools capability is invented.
- Dispatched naming passes an internal abort signal through pending admission;
  cancellation, timeout or early settlement aborts admission and stops owned
  readers. Late successful spawns retain stop ownership. Direct file/admission
  refusals resolve null immediately without waiting for a nonexistent exit.
  Output snippets, extraction text, returned titles and raw exception payloads
  are omitted from private-route diagnostics; unexpected errors retain fixed
  Sentry reports. Other-provider delivery paths are preserved.

### Coverage and ownership handoff

GT1-02-11D authors cases in the two existing naming suites; GT1-02-13 executes
those tests and type checks. The ordinary suite currently fully mocks shared
argument and SSH modules: extend those mocks to expose the real registry/builders
and remote destination helper for file cases. Successful direct spawn doubles
must return a positive PID and `success: true`; SSH preparation must return its
stdin script. Cover missing script/ownership, competing sources, selected
configuration snapshots during detector/preparation awaits, utility-provider
switches, explicit utility model versus provider default, session replacement and
blank account values, cancellation during admission, late success, early result,
spawn failure/exit and diagnostic exclusion. Retain legacy-provider cases.

GT1-08-14E remains the owner of effort retry provenance, validated fallback and
configuration identity across attempts. This packet does not change the retry
algorithm or claim it preserves a captured configuration across recursive retries.
Only the naming-consumer descriptions in five existing parity blockers changed;
all acceptance statuses, applicability, required flags and live authorization
remain unchanged. No public support documentation or capability promotion is
warranted before qualification.

merge base `0a56e0fa1c18e25a8e8a70dfc37378ae7d076b4f`; HEAD ahead/behind
**107/10**. No fetch, branch change or base mutation. Images analyzed: **0**.

Authored seven isolation cases in the single named suite,
`src/__tests__/main/agents/agent-completeness.test.ts`. The entire pre-existing
`Agent Completeness` describe body is byte-for-byte unchanged, preserving the
historical 101 baseline cases and subsequent registry assertions.

- A hoisted in-memory `electron-store` constructor reuses the existing Claude
  storage test's get/set/store pattern before application imports. Registration
  still constructs all eight real storage adapters, verified by class identity.
  A real Claude adapter origin/name round-trip exercises the memory boundary.
- A second guard spies on the underlying CommonJS `fs` object and its promise
  API, then synchronizes named ESM exports. Only source/dependency module reads
  are permitted; data reads, writes, directory creation and write-capable opens
  refuse before disk access. Import/registration and after-test assertions also
  catch swallowed access errors. The configured preferences root is excluded
  even when inherited XDG/APPDATA points into an otherwise allowed module root.
- The negative control bypasses only the Store mock, keeping the filesystem
  guard active. It uses the actual constructor options, with no forced cwd or
  project name. Installed `conf` derives the fallback name from its caller,
  `electron-store`; the assertion expects that preferences read to refuse even
  though Maestro's canonical resolver selects the stubbed `MAESTRO_USER_DATA`.
  Removing the Store mock therefore fails registration deterministically while
  the independent filesystem guard remains in place.
- String, Buffer and file-URL reads, synchronous writes/mkdir and promise
  reads/writes/mkdir have explicit refusal sentinels. Environment stubbing uses
  Vitest's restore API. No HOME/CODEX_HOME changes, fixture directories, native
  processes, credential reads or operator-store content inspections are needed.

Independent static review corrected the initially forced fallback project name
and confirmed no remaining blocking issues. Source changes are **290 additions /
2 deletions**, **292 changed lines**. Four directory-grant blocker descriptions
and P46's existing pending-check blocker now name this authored coverage; all
acceptance statuses, requirements, applicability and live authorization remain
unchanged. The baseline isolation blocker stays unresolved until GT1-02-13 passes

reported **108 task-only / 10 base-only** commits before this task.

Authored **27 regression cases** with **431 added test lines** in the single
named suite, `src/__tests__/renderer/components/Wizard/screens/AgentSelectionScreen/hooks.test.tsx`.
All previous test content is preserved byte-for-byte. Reused H's synthetic
Wizard state, existing agent fixture, real configuration/discovery hooks,
fake timers and in-memory IPC mocks; no new helper or production code.

- Executable clear ownership survives Grok/Codex round trips. Successful custom
  path saves and already-empty clears invalidate the old catalog after persistence
  and trigger discovery using the chosen path without changing model/effort text.
- Deferred configuration loads cannot select a stale owner, reopen its view or
  overwrite a new account/provider's manual values. Cases cover account changes,
  overlapping opens, panel close/reopen, full Wizard close/reopen and unmount.
- Pending model and both effort-key responses cannot restore old catalogs or
  discovered status after account/provider changes, close/reopen or unmount.
- Forced refresh completion cannot publish its payload or trigger extra discovery
  after owner changes, manual edits, close/reopen or unmount. The positive case
  proves a current successful refresh re-probes through the shared hook.
- Codex pending open/refresh responses retain the legacy three-argument API and
  cannot overwrite Grok after a switch. Existing Codex error assertions remain;
  new Grok refresh-error assertions cover local and SSH telemetry and retained text.

The UI reveals configuration controls only after the initial open finishes.
The delayed-config overwrite assertions therefore edit a newly opened owner
before the older request resolves, rather than inventing a first-load edit path.
Unmount checks cover observable callbacks/state and follow-up IPC; React's own
unmount protection means they do not independently prove every internal guard.

REG-MODEL and REG-DISCOVERY. Acceptance/applicability/status fields and task
owners remain unchanged. GT1-02-05H2 retains Auto Run worktree coverage;
